of exemplary life. The sea of knowledge has arrived. That fountain of goodness has arrived. That sea of humility has arrived." And saying these things, they rushed to welcome him. The great and the small greeted him, and even the noblewomen themselves rushed to greet him. And the venerable father, Abuna Damyanos, gathered them all in one place and began to instruct them as he was accustomed to do. And after having heard the word of his teaching from his mouth, some of them renounced marriage, others abandoned their own fiancée, others still left, some their wealth, some their house and some their field (Mt 19:29), some their herds and flocks and any property, as one reads in the Gospel of Luke where our Lord Jesus Christ himself teaches the crowds, saying: "I have brought fire to the earth and I want nothing other than that it be kindled; I have brought the sword and not peace, I have come to separate the father from the son and the mother from the daughter. The bride from her mother-in-law" (Lk 12:49, 53).

60 And in the same manner, those inhabitants of Hagara Masqal renounced everything they had and said to him: "O our father, we want you to give us the monastic habit, because if we do not become monks, we cannot save ourselves nor live." He replied and said to them: "Will you be able to persevere in the monastic life? For the monastic life is hard and its way is narrow (Mt 7:14)." They replied and said to him: "O our father, not only are we willing to accept the monastic life, but we are determined even to die (Lk 22:32; Acts 21:13)!" Having verified their resolve, he gathered those who wanted to embrace the monastic life and gave them the blessed scapular of our holy fathers. And every morning the number of those who became monks grew. In a single day, the quantity of those who consecrated themselves to the monastic life at Hagara Masqal reached the number of forty (monks), and the following day twenty. He recommended them to the rule he had taught them and

took his leave of them. 61 He then went to that monastery where they lived, and the name of the mountain was Semay Godaba. And he went away filled with great joy, like one who finds the booty of his enemy (Ps 119:162), and he gathered the flock that he had assembled through the preaching of tongues and introduced them into that sacred vestibule which is the monastic order given to them in trust. Those stars are Abba Qozmos and Abba Petros, Abba Abimos and Abba Margorawos and their son Tasfa Iyyasus and the three children that their sister Walatta Sayon bore; they are: the deacon Istifanos and the cantor Zakkaryas, and Yonas who

occupied himself with divine questions, according to what is written: "And I meditate on your commandments" (Ps 119:15). In those days, their mother Sosanna was at the convent of the widows together with her daughter Waletta Sayon, who imitated her in behavior and in every good work.

62 When that spiritual flock gathered, the chosen shepherd separated the men from the women. (He placed) the men in the male monastery and the women in the convent of the widows. They began (therefore to observe) the good order that resembles the order of the angels. The fame of the man of God, Abuna Damyanos, spread in all the lands of Sira. Indeed, many wonders were being accomplished through him: he healed the infirm, expelled demons, and cured those who had difficult diseases. His whole way of acting was peaceful, (it was carried out) in discernment, in wisdom. He indeed walked in the Spirit of healing (1 Cor 12:9), therefore strength sustained him. He strove for the salvation of souls according to what he had learned from the Prophets and the Apostles, preachers of justice. The prophets preached and labored in the teaching of the word (Ps 107:12), forbidding the worship of idols and imposing the adoration of the one God. And the Apostles were diligent in the teaching of the word: they moved to all the ends of the world (Mk 16:20) and taught the faith in the Father, in the Son, and in the Holy Spirit (Mt 28:19-20).
ወበእንተዝ ፡ ፈነወ ፡ መልእክተ ፡ ኅበ ፡ ዴገና ፡ መኩንነ ፡ ሲሬ ፡ እንዘ ፡ ይብል ፡ ሀበኒአ ፤ አሐተ"! ፡ ገዳመአ” ፡ እምአድባረ ፡ በድውአ” ፡ ከመአ ፡ እሕንፅአ ፡ በህየአ ፡ ኀበ ፡ አኀድርአ ፡ አባግዕየአ” ፡ እለ ፡ ኪያሆንአ ፡ ወሀበኒአ ፣ እግዚአብሔር ፡ ወእፈቅ
Abuna Demyanos decides to build a cenobium for his disciples. 63 And when this venerable father, Abuna Damyanos, saw that his children whom he had generated from the sacred womb had grown, he nourished them with the milk of the breasts of their holy mother, the Church. With the approval of his master and the elders of the cenobium, he decided to build a convent only for them, so that they could observe the rule like the one he had undertaken in the monastery of Abuna Muse. He had indeed left (that monastery) to grow (in perfection) and not to diminish; he had left to do penance and not to seek pleasures; he had left to take the narrow way in order to pursue the spacious one (Mt 7:13-14). He left to do penance and not to live luxuriously, and for this very reason there was a power that sustained him.

Abuna Demyanos sends a delegation to Degana. 64 He therefore sent a missive to Degana, prince of Sira, saying: "Grant me a secluded place among the uninhabited zones so that I may build there a dwelling for my flock that the Lord has given me. I want

to lead them into grassy pastures and into places with water (Ps 23:2) because I do not want them to suffer penury and for thirst to afflict them. I do not want them to become thin nor to languish, because if they become thin and waste away, he who gave them to me will be irritated against me and will reproach me. For this reason, I fear that the master of the sheep may say to me: 'Why have you sent my sheep to ruin?' and I will not be able to answer him. He is indeed the King of kings and the Lord of lords (1 Tm 6:15)." And the prince of Sira, having heard the word of the messengers of Abuna Damyanos, rejoiced and said to them: "Very well! Let it be for you according to your word! Choose for yourself a locality among all the hermitages that are under my jurisdiction, and the place that you choose shall be for you and for your descendants who will come after you!" The messengers returned and told Abuna Damyanos what the prince of Sira, Degana, had said to them. The venerable father rose and, looking toward heaven, offered thanksgiving (Mt 14:19; Jn 11:41) to his Creator because he accompanied him in all his ways. The choice falls on Zagar. Degana first objects then accepts.

65 Thereafter he said to his children: "My children, [what place is the best for us?". They replied: "We prefer such-and-such a hermitage." That hermitage was far away and was not in proximity to people. Those messengers went again to the prince of Sira, Degana, and said to him: "That place of the hermitage of Zager, I want that, and I desire that it become for me the place of my rest until my Lord whom I worship (Rom 1:9) wills, and will lead me to another destination that he wills." These words the messengers reported to the prince of Sira, Degana, who, after having heard them, clapped both his hands and said: "These men of Bur are truly naive beyond measure and behave like children!" He turned to those emissaries and asked them: "Our fathers, have you perhaps chosen the place of death? Has the Lord not been generous with me? Blessed be his name! Choose for yourselves what you want, as for this mountain of death and pain, do not desire (it). The book says indeed: 'Do not take me away in the middle of my life' (Is 38:10; Ps 102:24)." 66 But those messengers said to him: "O our Lord, as for death, we do not fear it at all; it is written indeed: 'Do not fear death, fear rather sin' because from death you will have no escape." And again he said: "Who is the man who can survive and not see death?" (Ps 89:48). The prince admired their determination and their answer, because

they had embraced death in the same way as life, so that he said to them: "(The place you have chosen) be for you a place of holiness and love, a place of blessing!" Those messengers then returned to the venerable father Abba Damyanos and reported to him what (the prince) had said to them and how he had granted them that place which was located on the western side of the country of Zager. On the way to Zagar. 67 They then took their leave of the people of that cenobium where they had lived. The monks of the place, who loved them so much for their good behavior, wept for them. They were indeed like gentle lambs (Mt 10:16; Jn 1:29) who had made their own the recommendation of our Lord who says: "Be simple as the dove and wise as the serpent" (Mt 10:16). And while they were taking their leave, they wept, saying: "Woe to us! Woe to us! O brothers rich in beauty, to whom do you abandon us? Woe to us! Woe to us! Brothers, chosen jewels, from now on our ornament has been torn away and removed. Woe to us! Woe to us! Behold how bare our country has remained!" And while they were saying this, they let tears flow from their eyes, they exchanged a spiritual greeting (Rom 16:16; 2 Cor 13:12) and then returned and re-entered their monastery and resumed their activities. 68 Those, instead, went on their way and reached that hermitage which was located on the western side of Zagar. And when they had contemplated it, they loved it and rejoiced at the beauty of its position. It was indeed detached from the villages of the men of the world. It was a silent place and its (geographical) position was convenient, its location pleasant. It was gratifying like the paradise of God. And they loved it with all their heart because it was far from their native country, and they remembered what our holy fathers said in the book of their chronicles: "If the monk, becoming a monk, renounces this world, let him go to a distant land where he cannot have news of his relatives and let him live there suffering hunger and thirst, in sweat and in labor, suffering cold and nakedness." In the new cenobium.
ወነበሩ ፡ ውስቴታኻ፤ ፡ ምስለ ፡ ዘከመዝ ፡ ግብር ፡ ቦእምኔሆሙ ፡ ቀዳሕያነ ፡ ማይ ፡ ወቦ ፡ መስተገብራነ ፡ ምድር ፡ ወቦ ፡ ሰፋይያነ ፡ አልባስ ፡ ዝውእቱ ፡ ግብረ ፡ መስፌ ። ወቦ ፡ ነጋድያንኞ ፡ ለመፍቅደ ፡ አኃው ፡ እመሂ ፡ ዘእክል ፡ አው ፡ ዘፄው ፡ እመሂ ፡ ዘማ እሰ ፡ ጠሊ ፡ ወእመሂ ፡ በዘኮነ ፡ መፍቅዳተ ፡ ማኅበር ፡ ሠርዖፆሙ ፡ ለኩሎሙ ፡ በአም
69 They lived in it in this manner: some of them became water carriers, others workers of the land, some still committed themselves to the making of clothes, which is the work of the needle. There were also those who dedicated themselves to trade for the needs of the brothers, some who

ጣነ ፡ ይትከሃሎሙ ። ወበኩሉ ፡ዘነበበ ፡ ይሴርሖ ፡ እግዚአብሔር ፡ ወአልቦ ፡ ዘይትዓዶ ፡ እምቃሉ ፡ በከመ ፡ ጽሑፍ ፡ ዘይብል ፡ ትእዛዘ ፡ ወሀቦሙ ፡ ወኢኃለፉ ።
occupied themselves with grain, some with salt, some with goat skins, and in any other need of the community he disposed them, according to the abilities of each one. In whatever he said, the Lord was at his side and there was no one who transgressed his word, as the Scripture says: "He gave them an order and"
they did not pass beyond it" (Ps 148:6).

### Heavenly Vision
ለእመ ፡ ነገርነሰ ፡ ጊሩቶሙ ፡ ዘአሜሃ ፡ ይነውኅ ፡ ነገር ፡ ወይከውን ፡ ዝንጋዓ ፡ ለሰማዕያን ፡ ወባሕቱ ፡ ለአሐቲሰ ፡ ርቱዕ ፡ ከመ ፡ እክሥታ ፡ እስመ ፡ ረባሕ ፡ ይእቲ ፡ ለዘ ፥ ይሰምዓ ። ይቤአ ፡ ፳ብእሴ ፡ እግዚአብሔር ፡ በእንተ ፡ እሉ ፡ አበዊነ ፡ እለ ፡ አሜሃ ፡ ቅዱሳን ፡ ደቂቀ ፡ ትምህርቱ ፡ ለአባ ፡ ድምያኖስ ፡ ይቤለኒ ፡ ይቤ ፡ ዘአ ስተርአየኒ ፤ በራእየ ፤ እግዚአብሔር ፡ ኦፍቁርየ ፡ ድምያኖስ ፡ እሉሰኬ ፡ መነኮሳት ፡ አስረፁ ፡ ክንፈ ፡ ከመ ፡ ንስር ፡ ወፈቀደ ፡ ከመ ፣ ይንሥኦሙ ፣ ወይምስጦሙ ፡ በሥጋ ሆሙ ፡ ወያንብሮሙ ፡ ኅበ ፡ ፈቀደ ፡ እስከ ፡ ሳዐ ፡ ዕድሜ ፡ ብሔረ ፡ እለ ፡ ከማሆሙ ፡ በከመ ፡ መሰቦ ፡ ለእዝራ ፡ ወአንበሮ ፡ ብሔረ ፡ እለ ፡ ከማሁ ። ወባሕቱ ፡፤ ኀደጎሙ ፡ ለዙሎሙ ፡፣ በእንተ ፡ ክልኤቱ ፡ ኑፉቃን ፡፣ እለ ፡ ሀለዎሙ ፣ ይትመየጡ ፡ ውስተ ፡ ብሔረ ፡ ዓለም ፡ ኦአብ ፡ አድኅኖ ፡ እምኔሃ ፡ ለይእቲ ፡ ምግባር ፡ አሜን ።
70 If our narrative concerning their probity of that time were to be (too) long and appear to the listeners [as a delirium, in any case it is right that I reveal one, because it will be of advantage to him who will hear it. There was a man of God who, regarding those fathers of ours of that time, the holy children of the teaching of Abba Damyanos, reports that (Abba Damyanos) confided to him what in turn had been revealed to him in a vision of God in which he was told: "O my beloved Damyanos, those monks have sprouted wings like the eagle (Is 40:31)! And He wanted to take them away and snatch them up in their bodies and place them in the place of His choosing, until the appointed time in the land of those like them, in the same way with which He had snatched up Ezra and had made him dwell in the land of those like him (4 Esd 14:49). Unfortunately, He left them all behind on account of those two unbelievers who were to return to the world. O Father, protect us from it, Amen!

### Ministry at Zager
ንግባእኬ ፡ ኅበ ፡ ጾታ ፡ ነገር ፡ ኀበ ፡ ናየድዕ ፡ ዜና ፣ ጊሩቱ ፡ ለአቡነ ፡ ድምያኖስዎ ፡ ዘከመ ፡ ነበረ ፡ ውስተ ፡ ሀገረ ፡ ዛግር ፡ ወመሀሮሙ ፡ በከመ ፡ ያለምድ ፡ ለዕድ ፡ ወለአንስት ፡ ቦዘኀደገ” ፡ ሐቲተ ፡ ማሪት ፡ ወቦዘኀደገ ፡ አጣዕዎ ፡ ወኩሎ” ፡ ግብረ ፡ ሰገል ። ወተማርዮ ፡ በዖፍ ፡ ወበኩሉ ፡ አራዊት ፡ ወቦእለ ፡ አኅደጎሙ ፡ አው ስቦ ፡ ዘእንበለ ፡ ሕግ ፡ ወዙሎ” ፡ ግብረ ፡ ዝሙት ፡ ወብዙኃን ፡ ይሰቀሉ ፡ ለአጽምኦተ ፡ ነገሩ ፤ እስመ ፡ በይዋሄ ፣ ይሜህሮሙ” ፡ እንዘ ፣ ያክሞስስ ፡፣ ወበእንተዝ ፡ ይበውእ ፡ ቃለ ፡ ትምህርቱ ፡ ውስተ ፡ ልቦሙ ፡ ከመ ፡ ማይ ፡ ቄሪር ፡ ውስተ ፡ አፈ ፡ ጽሙእ ፡ ወስዉጥ ፡ ውስተ ፡ አባሉ ፡ መንፈሰ ፡ ኃይል ፡ ወይሴርሖ ፡ ኅበ ፡ ኩሉ ፡ ዘፈቀደ ።
71 Let us therefore resume our discourse and announce the facts regarding the goodness of Abuna Demyanos when he was in the land of Zagar. According to his custom, he instructed men and women, so much so that there were those who abandoned the practice of consulting the fortune-teller, those who abandoned the worship of idols and every practice of divination, as well as the custom of making omens through birds or any other animal. There were also those whom he had made desist from entering into marriages without the law and every other work of fornication, and many remained enraptured in listening to his word (Lk 19:48). In fact, he instructed them with goodness and smiling, and for this reason the word of his teaching penetrated the heart like fresh water in the mouth of the thirsty. The Spirit of power was present in his body and accompanied him in every desire of his (Rom 1:10).

### Abuna Demyanos tames the rebellious ox
ወበአሐቲ ፡ ዕለት ፡ እመዋፅል ፡ እንዘ ፡ የሐውር ፡ ለምህሮ ፡ አሕዛብ ፡ ረከበ ፤ አሐደ ፡ ሐረሳዌ” ፡ እንዘ ፡ የሐርስ ፡ በጽንፈ ፡ ፍኖት ፡ ወላህም ፡ የአብዮ ፡ እስመ ፡ ወድቀ ፡ ወዔመ ፡ ማእከለ ፡ ገራህት ። ወአልዓለ ፡ ቃሎ ፡ ወከልሐ ፡ በቀጥዓ ፡ ወቀሰፎ ፡ በሐብል ፡ ወበዝኒ ፡ ኢይቤሎ ፡ ኦሆ ፡ ወነሥአ ፡ ኩናቶ ፡ ወሮፀ ፡ ወፈቀደ ፡ ከመ ፡ ይር ግዞ ፡ ወከልሐ ፡ ሎቱ ፡ አብ ፡ ክቡር ፡ አቡነ ፡ ድምያኖስ ፡ ወይቤሎ ፡ ወልድየ ፡ ኢትግ በር ፡ እኩየ ፡ ዲበ ፡ ላህምከ ፡ እስከ ፡ ትሬኢ ፡ ዕበየ ፡ እግዚአብሔር ። ወዘንተ ፡ ብሂሎ ፡
72 Once, while he was traveling for the instruction of the peoples, he came upon a farmer who was plowing on the edge of the road with an ox that was recalcitrant; the animal had in fact collapsed and was lying supine in the middle of the field. (The farmer) raised his voice and shouted with anger and struck it with the whip, but despite this, the ox did not heed him, so much so that he took his spear and lunged at the animal with the intention of

በጽሐ ፡ ኅበ ፡ ላህም ፡ ወይቤ ፡ ላህምኒ ፡ አእመረ” ፡ ዘአጥረዮ ፡ ወአድግኒ ፡ ምቅማኅ ፡ ወቤተ ፤ እግዚኡ ፡ ወዘንተ ፡ ብሂሎ ፡ ወረቀ ፡ ውስተ ፡ ገጹ ፡፤ ወቀብኦ ፡ ኩለንታሁ ፡ በትእምርተ ፡ መስቀል ፡ ወይቤ ፡ በስመ ፡ አብ ፡ ወወልድ ፡ ወመንፈስ ፡ ቅዱስ ፡ ተንሥእ ፡ ወኢተዐምዖ ፡ ለእግዚእከ ። ወበጊዜሃ ፡ ተንሥአ ፡ ውእቱ ፡ ላህም ፡ ፍጡነ ፡ ወሐረሰ ፡ ዘእንበለ ፡ ያጽንን ፡ ትልመ” ።
striking it. The venerable father, Abuna Damyanos, shouted at him, saying: "My son! Do not harm your ox until you have seen the greatness of God." Having said this, he approached the animal and said to it: "The ox recognized its owner and the donkey the pasture and the house of its master" (Is 1:3). After having said this, he spat in its face and anointed its whole body with the sign of the cross and said: "In the name of the Father, and of the Son, and of the Holy Spirit! Rise and do not rebel against your master." And at that moment the bovine rose
ወብዙኀ ፡ አንከረ ፡ ውእቱ ፡ ብእሲ ፡ 'እም ፡ ርኢየ”” ፡ ዝንቱ ፡ ተአምር ፡ ወይቤ ፤ ይእዜሰ ፡ ሐዋርያተ ፡ እግዚእ ፣፤ መጽኡ ፡ ኅቤየ ። ወእመ ፡ አኮ ፣ መላእክተ ፡ እግዚ አብሔር ፡ ወረዱ ፡ ኅበ ፡ ገራህትየ ። ወእምዝ ፡ አስተብቀ"ዖሙ ፡ ወይቤሎሙ ፡ ንግ ሩኒ ፤ አጋፅዝትየ ፡ አይቴ ፡ መካንክሙ ፡ ወምንት” ፡ ስመ ፡ መምህርክሙ ። ወይቤልዎ ፡ መካንነሰ ፡ በረካ” ፡ ሲሬ ፡ ወስመ ፡ አቡነሂ ፡ ድምያኖስ ፡ ወይቤሎሙ ፡ ባኡ ፡ ቤትየ ፡ ወኅደሩ፤ ፡ ወባርኩ ፡ ቤትየ ፡ ወብእሲትየ ፤ ወውሉድየ ፡ ወጥሪትየ ። ወአበይዎ' ፡ ወሐሩ ፤ ፍኖቶሙ ። ርእዩኬ ፡ ወአእምሩ ፡ ከመ ፡ ገባሬ ፡ ተአምር ፡ ወመንክር ፡ ውእቱ ፡ በሀገርኒ ፤ ወበገዳምኒ ፡ ከመ ፡ ሐዋርያት ፡ እንዘ ፣፤ ያኤምሮሙ!" ፡ በነገሩ ፡ ጸሎቱ" ፡ ተሀሉ ፡ ምስሌነ" ፡ አሜን።
immediately and plowed without distorting the furrow. 73 Upon seeing this miracle, that man remained greatly amazed and said: "So then, the Apostles of the Lord have come to me! Or even the angels of God have descended into my field." Afterward, he begged them, saying to them: "My lords, tell me where your dwelling is and what the name of your master is!" They answered him: "Our dwelling is found at Baraka of Sira and the name of our father is Damyanos." He said to them: "Enter my house and spend the night there (Acts 16:15)! Bless my house and my wife, my children, and my possessions." But they refused and continued on their way. Observe then and know how he was a worker of miracles and wonders, in the cities and in the countryside, who instructed (his listeners) with his word, like the Apostles. May his prayer be with us, Amen!

### Abuna Demyanos confronts a lion
ወካዕበ ፡ በአሐቲ ፡ ዕለት ፡ እመዋዕል ፡ እንዘ ፡ የሐውር ፡ ውስተ ፡ ፍኖት ፡ አቡነ ፡ ድምያኖስ ፡ ምስለ ፤ አርድእቲሁ ፡ ወአድግ ፤ ምስሌሆሙ ። ወተንሥአ ፡ አንበሳ' ፡ ወሰረረ ፤ ወተጽእኖ ፡ ለአድግ ፡ ወርእዮ ፡ አብ ፡ ክቡር ፡ አቡነ ፤ ድምያኖስ ፡ ከመ ፡ ተጽዕኖ ፡ አንበሳ ፡ ለአድግ ፡ ይቤሎ ፡ ለውእቱ ፡ አንበሳ ፡ ረድ ፡ በቃለ ፡ እግዚአብሔር ፡ እምላዕለ ፡ አድግ ፡ ወኢታሕስም ፡ ላዕሌሁ ፡ ወተመይጠ ፡ ይምነ ፡ ወጽግመ ፡ እስመ ፡ ፈርሀ ፡ እምድምፀ ፡ ቃለ ፡ ግርማሁ ፡ ለብእሴ ፤ እግዚአብሔር ፡ ወወረደ ፡ ዘእንበለ ፡ ያሕስም ፡ ላዕሌሁ ፡ ወኢነሰኮ ፡ አላ ፡ ጐየ ፡ እምኔሁ ፡ ከመ ፡ ከልብ ፡ ዘሰደዶ ፡ ነባሬ ፡ ባዕድ ፡ ወየ ፡ እስከ ፡ ኢይትረከብ ፡ አሰሩ ፡ ወአንከሩ ፡ እለ ፡ ምስሌሁ ፡ ሶበ ፡ ርእዩ ፡ ዘንተ ፡ ዓቢየ ፡ መንክረ ፡ ወይቤሉ ፡ ዮምኒ ፡ ርኢነ ፡ ዕፁበ ፡ ግብረ ። አንበሳኒ ፡ ይትኤ ዘዝ ፡ ሎቱ።
74 Another time, Abuna Damyanos was traveling together with his disciples and with them there was a donkey. A lion rose up and climbed with force onto the back of the donkey, and the venerable father Abuna Damyanos saw how the lion had loaded itself onto the donkey. He said to that lion: "By the word of God, get down from the donkey and do not harm it!" (The lion) turned to the right and to the left, for it was afraid of the voice of the powerful word of the man of God. It then climbed down without causing it harm. It did not bite it but ran away from him like a dog that is put to flight by a servant. And it ran so far that not even its tracks were found. At the sight of such a miracle, those who were with him were very astonished and said: "Today we have seen a wondrous work! Even the lion obeys him (Mk 4:41)." God establishes a covenant with Abuna Damyanos
ንትመየጥኬ ፡ ኅበ ፡ ዜና ፡ ነገሩ ፡ ለብእሴ ፡ እግዚአብሔር ፡ ወንንግር ፡ ዘከመ ፡ ተውህቦ ፡ ኪዳነ ፡ እምኅበ ፡ እግዚአብሔር ፡ይቤ ፡ 8እምነ ፡ እሙንቱ ፡ ከዋክብት ፡እለ ፡ ዘከርነ ፡ ስሞሙ ፡ በርእሰ ፡ መጽሐፍ ፡ አመ ፡ ደወየ ፡ደዌ ፡ ዘለሞት ፡ ወመሰጡ ፡ ነፍሶ ፡ መላእክት ፡ ወአብጽሕዎ ፡ ቅድመ ፡ እግዚአብሔር ፡ወአርአይዎ ፡ ኩሎ" ፡ ሀብታተ ፡
75 Let us resume again telling the story of the man of God and narrate how a covenant was granted to him by God. One of those stars whose name we remembered at the beginning of the book fell ill with a mortal infirmity, and the angels took his soul and led him into the presence


[p. 196] {#po.50.p196}
ዘይትወሀብ ፡ ለኅሩያነ ፡ ልዑል ፡ ወነበረ ፡ መጠነ ፡ ሠሉስ ፡ መዋዕል ፡ ወአይድዕዎ ፡ ለአቡነ ፡ ድምያኖስ ፡ ከመ ፡ ደወየ ፡ ውእቱ ፡ ብእሴ ፤ እግዚአብሔር ፡ ወይቤልዎ ፡ ነዓአ ፡ እስመአ ፡ ይደዊአ ፡ እጐነአ ፡ አቢሞስአ ፡ ደዌአ ፡ ዘለሞትአ ፡ ወባሕቱአ ፡ ነዓአ ፡ ጸሊ ፡ ላዕሌሁ ። ወተንሥአ ፡ ፍጡነ ፡ ወሖረ ፡ እንዘ ፡ ይጐጐዕ ፡ ወበጽሐ ፡ ኅበ ፡ ይደዊ ፡ ውእቱ ፡ ብእሴ ፤ እግዚአብሔር ፡ ወጸውዖ ፡ ወይቤ ፡ አቢሞስ'! ፣ አቢሞስ ፡ ወይቤ ፡ እግዚእየ ፡ ወይቤሎ ፡ ትመውትኑነ ፡ ዘእንበለ ፡ ተስፋ" ፡ ለአኃዊከ ፡ ወሶበ ፡ ይቤሎ ፡ ከመዝ ፡ ተኀየለ ፡ እምውስተ ፡ ዘይሰክብ ።
of the Lord and showed him all the graces that are bestowed upon the elect of the Most High, and he remained there for three days. They then announced to Abuna Damyanos that that man of God was ill and said to him: "Come, because our brother Abimos is ill, a sickness with danger of death. Come at least to pray for him." He rose promptly, went in haste, and arrived at that man of God who was lying ill. He called him and said: "Abimos! Abimos." And he said: "My Lord!" He said to him: "Do you want to die without (giving?) hope to your brothers?" When he said this to him, while remaining in the bed, he was strengthened.

76 He then began to recount how he had had the divine vision that would be for the benefit of many. And he said: "I saw a luminous man who, by the brilliance of his light, dazzled the eyes. His throne was higher than all the others I saw and resembled a king. Again I saw someone who bore his image and his likeness, adorned with a radiant beauty. And I saw a third who was similar to them in beauty and in features, and there was no defect in them, and they rejoiced each in their own dwelling (Jn 14:2), together with their children in luminous habitations. Afterward, I saw yet a fourth land that, as for appearance, was as pleasant as the others. Its foundations were according to the heavenly model (Ex 25:9) and it awaited the coming of the elect man. Sparkling stars rotate around that city and await the coming of the sun. And to the Lord of spirits they said: 'Bring us our father so that we may rejoice and taste the beauty of his stay.' And He said to them: 'Have a little patience with me until the measure of those after you is filled.' 77 'The one I saw first in the image of a king was our father Ewostatewos, who abounded in brilliance fully seven times more than the sun (Dan 12:3; Mt 13:43). The one of whom I said 'the following' is our father Absadi, he who had received the responsibility of his father Ewostatewos. The one of whom I said 'the third of them' is our father Muse. The fourth city, with its comely and pleasant appearance, is your city, and its stars, those who have begged the Lord of spirits for the sun to come, are your children who await your coming, because among them you are like the sun. And I also heard that the Lord of spirits was saying to you: 'I have sworn to you in my powerful name that every person who believes in the word and converts and receives from your hands the holy things, thanks to you, will be saved.' I swear to you, O my beloved Damyanos, that whoever will make your memorial and will entrust himself to your prayer and will have had the book of your struggle written, or will write it himself, will be saved by your merit. This is the end of my discourse on earth. I am in fact in a hurry to go to my eternal rest, the land of joy and pleasure, the land of relief and rest where there is neither groaning nor crying, the land of the inheritance that

ወፍግዓ ፡ ብሔረ ፡ ናህይ ፡ ወዕረፍት ፡ ኀበ ፡ አልቦ ፡ ናእክ ፡ ወገዓር ፡ ምድረ” ፡ ርስት ፡ ዘኢርእይዎ ፡ ዓይነ ፡ ንስር ። ወዘንተ ፡ ብሂሎ ፡ ኖመ ፡ ወሖረ ፡ ውስተ ፡ ሕይ ወት ፡ ዘለዓለም ፤ በረከቱ” ፡ ትኩን ፡ ምስሌነ”! ፡ አሜን” ።
not even the eye of the eagle can see (ParJer 10:24)." And having said this, he fell asleep and went to eternal life. May his blessing remain with us, Amen!

### Abuna Demyanos and the young man who received a miracle
'ወብየ ፡ ካዕበ” ፣ ካልእ” ፣፡ ስምዕ” ፣ እስመ ፣ ጽሑፍ ፡ በስምዐ ፡ ክልኤቱ ፡ ወሠለስቱ ፡ ይቁም ፡ ነገር ። ወእንዘ ፡ የሐውር ፡ ካዕበ ፡ አቡነ ፡ ድምያኖስ ፡ ለምህሮ ፡ አሕዛብ ፤ ዘበሐውርተ ፡ ዛግር ፡ ረከቦ ፡ በፍኖት ፡ ለ ወሬዛ ፡ እምሰብአ ፡ ዓለም ፡ ይቤሎ ፡ አቡነ ፡ ድምያኖስ ፡ ለከ ፡ እብለከ ፡ ኦወሬዛ ፡ ለእመ ፡ ብከ ፡ መብልዕ" ፡ አብልዓነ ፡ ወለእመ ፡ አልብከሰ ፡ ንሕነ ፡ ናብልዐከ ። ወዘንተ ፡ ሰሚዖ ፡ ተከዘ ፡ ወሬዛ ፡ እስመ ፡ መሰሎ ፡ በእንተ ፡ መብልዐ ፡ እክል ፡ ዘይቤሎ ፡ ወይቤሎሙ ፡ አጋዕዝትየ ፡ ምንት” ፡ ብየ ፡ ውስተ ፡ ዝ ፡ ገዳም ፤ መሥንቅኒኻ ፡ አልብየ ፡ ዘኅብስት ፡ ወዝቅኒ ፡ አልብየ ፡ ዘስታይ ፡ ወሀገርየኒ ፡ ርኅቅት ፡ ከመ ፡ ተግኅሥ” ፡ ኅበ ፡ ገብርክሙ ፡ ወአኮ ፡ ዘትበጽሕዋ ፡ ፍጡነ ። ወአንትሙኒ ፡ ከመ ፡ ታብልዑኒ ፡ መስንቅ ፣ ወዝቅ" ፡ አል ብክሙ ፡ እምአይቴ ፡ ለክሙ ፡ ዘታበልዑኒ ። ወአውሥአ” ፡ረባን ፡ አብ ፡ ክቡር ፡አባ፣ ድምያኖስ ፡ ወይቤሎ ፡ አኮ ፡ ክመ ፡ በኅብስት ፡ ዘየሐዩ ፡ ሰብእ ፡ አላ ፡ በኩሉ ፡ ቃል ፤ ዘይወፅእ ፡ እምአፉሁ ፡ ለእግዚአብሔር ። መብልዕሰ ፡ ነገረ ፡ እግዚአብሔር ፡ ዘያጸግብ ፡ ነፍስኒ ፤ ወሥጋኒ ፡ ኅቡረ ፡ ወዘይሬስዮሙ ፤ ለምድራውያን ፡ ሰማያውያን ፡ ትክል ፡ ወታሠልጥ ፡ ወትባልሕ ፡ እምሲኦል ፡ ታሕቲት ።
78 I have yet another testimony, because it is written: "Every matter shall be established on the testimony of two or three witnesses" (Dt 19:15; Mt 18:17). While Abuna Damyanos was going again to instruct the peoples of the villages of Zager, along the way a young man from the people of the world crossed his path. Abuna Damyanos said to him: "I say to you, young man! If you have food, let us eat; if you do not have any, we will make you eat." Having heard this, the young man became sad because he believed he was speaking to him of material food. He then said to them: "My lords, what could I have in this desert place? I have no bag for bread, nor wineskin for drinks, and since my country is far away, you cannot stop at your servant's, you could not in fact reach it in haste. Neither can you give me to eat because you have neither bag nor wineskin. Where do you have something to be able to give me to eat?" The master, the venerable father, Abba Damyanos, answered and said to him: "'No person lives ever by food, but by every word that comes out of the mouth of God' (Dt 8:3; Mt 4:4). The (true) food is the word of God that satiates the soul and the body together and that makes the terrestrial heavenly; (the word of God) is capable, effective, and saves from the depths of the underworld."

79 Having said this, he expounded to him words from the sacred books of the Old and New Testaments. He told him that those who do good will be saved, while those who do evil will be condemned (Mt 16:27; 25:46; Jn 5:29). When he heard these words, he had fear and trembling. In fact, until that moment he had never heard anything of the kind. He remembered his sins that he had committed before then and wept in his inner self. He then took leave of them and went on his way, while they went on their way. When that man separated from them and was still on the path, a snake coiled around him and bit him. They led him, carrying him, and he reached the country. He approached death, and the angels of darkness seized him and carried away his soul into the sufferings of the underworld. 80 The venerable father, Abuna Damyanos, realized it and, riding a chariot of the winds, reached the place where the souls were staying. He then stood before the Lord of spirits and spoke with vigor and said: "Remember, O Lord, the covenant that you swore to me, in which you said: 'Whoever will have heard the word of your teaching that emanates from your mouth will be saved because of you.'" The Lord of spirits said: "Heaven and earth will pass away, but my words will not pass away (Mt 5:18). In truth I tell you, I seek

in fact a foothold to save people." He then said to the angels of light: "Bring this soul to eternal life because it has heard the word of my beloved Damyanos!" And the angels did as the Lord of spirits had said and snatched that soul from the dark spirits and led it to eternity because it had heard the word of Abuna Damyanos. 81 After this vision, his soul returned and rejoined his body and he breathed. Those who were weeping fell silent, and he said to them: "Do not weep for me, weep rather for yourselves (Lk 23:28) and have fear of the condemnation that I have been able to see. As for me, I have escaped through the prayer of the man of God, the monk of such-and-such a monastery, Abba Demyanos, who has had from God a covenant as a gift." He then told them of their meeting along the road and of the useful things he had said to him, of how the angels of darkness had seized him and led him to the place of tears and the gnashing of teeth (Mt 25:30). (He told) of how the man of God Abuna Damyanos came before the Lord of spirits and spoke with strength. He told them from beginning to end all these things that were accomplished thanks to the prayer of the man of God Abuna Damyanos.

82 And when he had finished his discourse, he said to his relatives: "By the living God, I implore you not to bury my body in any place except at the monastery of the man of God, by whose prayer my life was saved." And after having said this, he expired, and his relatives carried out his request. They took charge of his corpse and led it to that monastery and told the monks everything that had happened. The brothers rejoiced to hear this miracle and the covenant that had been concluded with their father, and the vision of Abimos, man of God, was fulfilled. But the venerable father Abuna Demyanos did not marvel and was not astonished at all the things that had been accomplished for him; he rendered his soul rather like refuse (Phil 3:8) to be thrown into the streets, remembering the saying: "He who will leave his soul for me will find it" (Mt 16:25). He endured affliction, and furthermore, he performed the good of his neighbor. Abuna Demyanos, man of charity 83 One day a man presented himself to him who was in difficult conditions and manifested to him his needs and the indigence of his house and the misery of his children and his wife. At that moment (the monks) had nothing to eat except a little grain that the steward had set aside for the commemoration. He went to the steward and, according to his custom, said to him: "My son, do you have something to give as alms? It is in fact favored before the Lord." He answered him: "O father, today there is really nothing except


[p. 202] {#po.50.p202}
ቤሎ ፡ ዮምሰ ፡ ኦአባ ፡ አልቦ ፡ ዘእንበለ ፡ ሕዳጥ ፡ እክል ፡ እለ ፡ ኀባእኩ ፡ ከመ ፡ ኢይጸራዕ ፡ ተዝካረ ፡ መምህርከ ፡ ወአድግኒ ፡ ዘይትለአክ ፡ ለቅዱሳን ፡ ወባዕድሰ ፡ አልቦ ። ወእምዝ ፡ ጸውዖ ፡ ለውእቱ ፡ እንግዳ ፡ ወይቤሎ ፡ በብሩህ ፡ ሕሊና ፡ ወበብሩህ ፡ ወፍሠሕች ፡ ልብ ፡ ወልድየ ፡ ኅረይ ፡ ለከ ፡ እምዝ ፡ እክል ፡ ወእምነ ፡ ዝንቱ ፡ አድግ ፡ ዘአቅረበ ፡ ለነ ፡ እግዚአብሔር ። ወነሥአ ፡ ዘኃረየ ፡ ወተፈጸመ ፡ በላዕሌሁ ፡ ዘይቤ ፡ አቡነ ፡ ጴጥሮስ ፡ በሲኖዶሱ ፡ ወልድየ ፡ አፍቅር ፡ ቢጸከ ፡ ከመ ፡ ነፍስከ ።
a little grain that I have hidden so that the memory of your master would not be neglected. We also have the donkey that is at the service of the saints. We have nothing else." Afterward, he called that guest and with a clear conscience, and with a luminous and joyful heart, said to him: "My son, choose between this grain and this donkey that God has placed at our disposal!" He took what he had chosen, and what our father Peter says in his Sinodos was fulfilled in him: "My son, love your neighbor as yourself" (Lev 19:18; Mt 5:43; 22:39).

### Abuna Demyanos in the Hamasen encounters robbers
ወካዕበ ፡ በአሐቲ ፡ ዕለት” ፡ እመዋፅል ፡ ሖረ ፡ አብ ፡ ክቡር ፡ ሀገረ ፡ ሐማሴን ፡ ከመ ፡ ይንግድ ፡ ለመፍቅዶደ ፡ አኃው ፡ እስመ ፡ ይትለአኮሙ” ፡ ከመ ፡ አሐዱ ፡ እምኔሆሙ ። ወእንዘ ፡ የአቱ ፡ ነሚኦ ፡ መፍቅደ ፡ አኃው ፡ ተንሥኡ ፡ ላዕሌሁ ፡ ፈያት ፡ ወሄድዎ ፡ ወውእቱ ፡ ተዘከረ ፡ ቃለ ፡ መድኃኒነ ፡ ዘይቤ ፤ በወንጌል ፡ ለዘይፈቅድ”ሞ ፡ ይትዓገልከ ፤ ኢትክልኦ ። ወበእንተዝ ፡ ኢከልኦሙ ፡ አላ ፡ ይቤሎሙ ፡ ዝንዋይ ፡ ዘአዕ ሩግ ፡ ወሕፃናት ፡ በጽሐክሙኑ ፡ ወይቤልዎ ፡ እወ ፡ በጽሐነ ፡ ሖር” ፡ ፍኖተከ ፡ ወሖረ ፡ ፍኖቶ ፡ እንዘ ፡ ይትፌሣሕ ፡ ከመ ፡ ዘተጸገወ ።
84 Again, one time the venerable father went to the land of the Hamasen to do some trading and use the proceeds for the needs of the brothers. In fact, he served them as if he were one of them. And while he was returning home with the provisions for the brothers, some bandits rose up against him and robbed him. But he remembered the word of our Savior who in the Gospel says: "Do not resist him who wants to strip you" (Mt 5:40). For this reason, he did not offer them resistance but said to them: "These things are for the elderly and the children, have they reached you?" They answered him: "Of course they have reached us. Continue on your way." He then continued on his path, rejoicing as if he had obtained gifts.
ወእሙንቱ” ፡ አብዳን ፤ ተፈሥሑ ፡ በአሕጐሎ ፡ ነፍሶሙ ። ወባሕቱ ፡ ይቤ ፡ ፳ እምኔሆሙ ፡ ሊተሰ ፡ ይፈርሃኒ ፡ ልብየ ፡ 'እም ፡ ነገረዝ ፣ መነኮስ” ፡ 'እስመ ፡ ኢሰማዕኩ ፡ እምአመ ፡ ወፃእኩ ፡ ለፈይቶ" ፡ ዘከመዝ” ። ወይእዜኒ ፡ ናግብእ ፡ ሎቱ ፡ ከመ ፡ ኢትርከበነ ፡ እኪት” ፡ በጸሎቱ ። ወይቤልዎ ፡ሀብ ፡ መክፈልተከ” ፡ ዘይረክበከ ፡ ንሕነሰ ፡ ኢናገብእ ፡ ሎቱ ። ወሖሩ ፡ ውስተ ፡ ፍኖቶሙ ። ወበጽሑ ፡ ማኅደሪሆሙ፤ ፡ ውስተ ፡ መካነ ፡ በድው ፡ ወጠብሑ ፡ ላህመ ፡ ወበልዑ ፡ እምኔሁ ፡ ኩሎሙ ። ወአኀ ዞሙ ፡ ዓቢይ ፡ ደዌ ፡ ዝውእቱ ፡ ሕማመ ፡ አማዑት ፡ ወሞቱ ፡ ኩሎሙ ፡ በውእቱ ፡ ሕማመ ፡ አማዑት ፡ ዘእንበለ ፡ ውእቱ ፡፣ ዘይቤ ፡ ናግብእ ፡ ሎቱ ። ዝኒኬ ፡ ዓቢይ ፡ ወመንክር ፡ ውእቱ ፡ እስመ ፡ ተሰብሐ ፤ እግዚአብሔር ፡ በሞተ ፡ ጸላእቱ ፡ በከመ ፡ ተሰብሐ ፡ በድቀተ ፡ ፈርዖን ፡ ወሰራዊቱ ፡ ወበሞተ ፡ ኢልዛቤል” ፣ ብእሲተ” ፤ አክ ዓብ” ፡ በቀለ” ፡ ናቡቴ ፡ ኅሩይ” ፡ ቤቱ?” ፡ ሎቱ ፡ ስብሐት ፡ ለዓለመ ፡ ዓለም ፡ አሜን ።
85 Even those fools rejoiced for the destruction of their soul. But one of them exclaimed: "My heart is trembling inside me on account of the words of this monk. In fact, since I gave myself to robbery, I have never heard such a thing. So then, let us return (his things) to him, because something sinister might happen to us through his prayer." They answered him: "Return your part; as for us, we will return nothing to him." They thus set off on their way and arrived at their dwellings in an uninhabited place. They slaughtered an ox and all ate its meat, and they were struck by a great sickness that was a sickness of the intestines. They all died from that sickness of the intestines, except the one who had urged them to return (the stolen goods) to him. This too is a great wonder, because the Lord was glorified with the death of his enemies, as He was glorified for the fall of Pharaoh and his army (Ex 15:1-21) and for the death of Jezebel, wife of Ahab, who died to avenge Naboth, the elect of his house (2 Kgs 9:30-37). To Him be glory for ages of ages, Amen!

### Summary
ወዝንቱሰ ፡ አብ ፡ ክቡር ፡ ዘንተ ፡ ኩሎ ፡ እንዘ ፡ ይትገበር ፡ ሎቱ ፡ አኮ ፡ ዘያፈ ቅር ፡ ተወድሶ ፡ አላ ፡ ይብላ” ፡ ለነፍሱ ፡ ተዘከሪ ፡ ዘጽሑፍ ፡ ኢትትፈሣሕ ፡ በድቀተ ፡
86 And this venerable father, while obtaining the grace to do all these things, did not love praises but repeated to his soul: "Remember that it is written: 'do not rejoice at the fall of your enemy' (Prov 24:17)." He also remembered


[p. 204] {#po.50.p204}
ጸላኢከ ። ወካዕበ ፡ ተዘከረ” ፣ ዘይቤ ፤ መድኃኒነ ፡ አፍቅሩ ፣ ጸላእተክሙ ፡ ወሠናየ ፡ ግበሩ ፤ ለእለ ፡ ይትዔገሉክሙ ፡ ወደኃርዎሙ ፡ ለእለ ፡ ይረግሙክሙ ፡ ዘንተኒ ፡ ወዘ ይመስሎ ፡ ያዜክራ ፡ ለነፍሱ” ። ወይመርሐ ፤ ውስተ ፡ ኩሉ ፡ ምግባረ ፡ ሠናይ ። ወለባዕዳንኒ ፡ ይሜህር ፡ ዘከመ ፡ ይገብር ፡ በረከቱ ፡ ትኩን ፣ ምስሌነ ፡ አሜን ።
that our Savior said: "Love your enemies and do good to those who mistreat you and bless those who curse you" (Lk 6:26-27). He reminded his soul of this and similar things and oriented it to perform every good work. And he taught others what he himself did. May his blessing be with us, Amen!

### Goodness of Abuna Damyanos with a sick person
ወለውእቱ ፡ ወሬዛ ፤ ዘመጽአ ፤ ለከሚተ ፡ አበሳሁ ፡፤ ተቀበሎ ፡ ሠናየ ፡ ተቀብ ሎተ ፡ ወኃዘነ ፡ በእንተ ፡ ደዌሁ ፡ እንተ ፡ ረከበቶ ፡ ወአሠነየ ፡ ላዕሌሁ ፡ በዘይትከሀሎ ፡ ወይጹሊ ፡ ላዕሌሁ ፡ ወይቀብኦ ፡ ቅብአ ፡ ጸሎት ፡ ወይገብር ፡ ሎቱ ፡ መፍቅዳተ ፡ ለከ ርሥ ፡ እመኒ ፡ ዘመብልዕ ፡ ወእመኒ ፡ ዘመስቴ ። እስከ ፡ ሐይወ ፡ በመንፈሰ ፡ አሶት ፡ ዘላዕሌሁ ፤ ወእምድኅረ ፡ ሐይወ ፡ ሖሩ ፡ ኅበ ፡ ሰብእ ፡ እንዘ ፡ ያነክር ፡ ግብረ ፡ እግዚ አብሔር ፡ ዘገብረ ፡ በላዕለ ፡ እደ ፡ ገብሩኞ ፡ አቡነ ፡ ድምያኖስ ፡ ጸሎቱ ፡ ትብጽሐነ” ፡ አሜን ።
87 And he granted a good welcome to that young man who had come to reveal his sin. He became sad for the sickness that was afflicting him and took care of him, according to his possibilities, praying for him and anointing him with the ointment of prayer (Jas 5:13-14) and attended to his nutritional needs, both for eating and for drinking, until he regained his health through the spirit of healing that was upon him. After he was healed, he went among the people, exalting the work that God had accomplished by the hand of His servant Abuna Damyanos. May his prayer reach us, Amen!

### Abuna Demyanos and the monster of Aramo
ወካዕበ ፡ በአሐቲ ፡ ዕለት ፤ እመዋዕል ፡ እንዘ ፡ ይሜህር ፡ በሀገረ ፡ መስቀል ፡ ዘውእቱ ፡ አረሞኞ፤ ፡ ዜነዎ ፡ እመሃይምናን ፡ ከመ ፡ሀሎ ፡ ፳”ብእሲ ፡ ዘኢየአምሮ ፡ ለእግዚአብሔር ፡ ወባሕቱ ፡ ኮነ ፡ ንብረቱ ፡ ውስተ ፡ ኩሉ ፡ ምርዓት ፡ ወርኩስ ፡ ወዝሙት ፡ ወቀቲለ ፡ ነፍስ ፡፤ ወስርቅ ፡ ወትዕግልት ፡ ወሀይድ ፡ ወሐቲተ ፡ ማሪት ፡ ወተጠይሮ ፡ በዖፍ ፡ ወበኩሉ ፡ አራዊት ። ወዓዲ ፡ ይቤ ፡ ለሊሁ ፡ ሶበ ፡ ሞተት ፡ ብእሲትየ ፡ ዘሔማየ ፡ ኀድገት ፡ ሊተ ፡ ክልኤ ፡ አዋልደ ፡ እለ ፡ ወለደቶን' ፡ መንታ ፡ ወአክረይኩ ፡ ላቲ ፡ መቃብረ ፡ ወእቤ ፡ አርኅቡ ፡ ወኢታጽብቡ ፡ መቃብረ ፡ ወገብሩ ፡ ሊተ ፡ በከመ ፡ ፈቀድኩ ። ወአምጽኡ ፡ በድነ ፡ ወእቤሎሙ ፡ ለሕፃናትሂ ፡ ቅብርዎን ፡ ምስለ ፡ እሞን ፡ ሕያዋኒሆን ፡ እንዘ ፡ ይበክያኞ ፡ ወአምጽእዎን” ፣ በከመ ፡ እቤሎሙ ፡ ወቀበርዎን ፡ ምስለ ፡ እሞን ፣ ሕያዋኒሆን ። ወባዕደኒ ፡ ብዙኃ ፡ ኃጢአተ ፡ ዘይገብር ፡ ዘዕፁብ ፡ ለነጊር ። ወአንተ ፡ ለእመ ፡ ሖርከ ፡ ኀቤሁ ፡ እምሰምዐ ፡ ቃለከ” ፡ ወእም ተመይጠ ፡ እምዝ ፡ ኩሉ ፡ ዘንቤ ። ወሶበ ፡ ሰምዓ ፡ አቡነ ፡ ድምያኖስ ፡ ይቤሎ ፡ ለዘነ ገሮ ፡መኑ ፡ ስሙ ፡ ወይቤሎ ፡ ጌሶ ፡ ወይብልዎ ፡ ስሙሰ ፡ ወይቤሎሙ ፡ አብ ፡ ክቡር ፡ ለደቂቁ ፤ መልዑ” ፡ ንሑር ፡ ውሉድየ ። በጊዜሃ ፡ ተንሥኡ ፡ ወሖሩ” ፡ ወበጽሑ ፡ ኅበ ፡ ሀገሩ ፡ ለውእቱ ፡ ብእሲ ፡ ወረከብዎ ፡ ሎቱ ፡
88 It happened then that one time, while he was teaching at Hagara Masqall, that is, Aramo, one of the faithful told him that there was a man who did not know God and not only that: he was a man who lived in every sort of lust and impurity, in fornication, in murder, in theft, in robbery, in extortion, in consulting the fortune-teller, in seeking omens through birds (Lev 19:26) and any other animal. Furthermore, he himself said: "When my legitimate wife died, she left me two twin daughters. I had a tomb dug for her and said: 'Widen and do not narrow the tomb!' and they carried out my order. They then brought the corpse, and I said to them: 'Bury the little girls alive together with their mother.' And as I had requested, they brought them weeping and buried them alive together with their mom." He also committed many other sins (very) difficult to tell. (That faithful said to him:) "If you were to go to him, he would listen to your word and would convert from all the things that we are saying." Having heard this, Abuna Demyanos said to the one who had told him: "What is his name?" He answered him: "To call him, they call him Heso." 89 The venerable father said to his children: "Come on, my children, let us go." They rose immediately and went and arrived at the country of that man and met him. He greeted them and they did the same, and the Holy Spirit descended

upon him as in Armis in the land of the priests. He entertained them and said to them: "From what country are you and from whom have you come?" They answered him: "We are looking for Heso!" He replied: "Do you know him?" They answered him: "We must know him!" He said to them: "Withdraw to the shade." They placed themselves in the shade, and he left them in the shade and went where he would prepare dinner for them. He then returned and asked them many questions and again said to them: "If you are looking for Heso, Heso is me!" And Abuna Damyanos said to him: "Your name, Heso, is correct. From now on, for you there will be something better (than in the past)." After having said this, he indicated to him the best remedy that would bring him benefit and whose conclusion would be happy. He accepted with fear and trembling. However, he asked him and said to him: "Is there or is there not a fair judgment before God?"

90 He answered him: "The judgment exists and there is a just judge." He said to him: "If therefore there is a fair justice, why ever condemn me who am made in a different way from those who know the Books and I have not had anyone who has taught them to me? Is it possible that (the judge) demands what He has not entrusted to me (Mt 25:24-25)? Will He perhaps condemn me to whom He has not granted teachers like you?" The venerable father answered and said to him: "This word that He has uttered through me will judge you." The young man replied: "Since I have committed so many errors, what will become of (my) past?" The venerable father answered and said to him: "I will make myself guarantor for the past things so that you will not be accused of them, if you confide them to me with honesty." The young man said: "Let it happen in me as you tell me." He then revealed his sin, repented of it, and separated himself from all those evil works that we have mentioned previously. He placed himself on the straight path in which the Lord is pleased, so much so that he reached monasticism. He pleased the Creator God with a good life. He scattered all his possessions in alms and welcomed poverty together with patience; he scourged himself three hundred times every hour and made so many prostrations to the point that blood flowed from his face like water. He did not take food and did not drink water except on Saturday and Sunday. He rested in glory, doing similar works. May his blessing remain upon us, Amen!

### Summary

91 And Abuna Damyanos lived bound to supplication, to prostration, and to fasts, keeping watch and practicing abstinence. He lived in fasting and in prayer, in admonition and in teaching, putting the teachings into practice with humility, together with meekness and love. These things and others similar

to them he entrusted to his children, just as he had learned them himself from the Holy Spirit and had guarded them until the day of his death.

### In search of a successor

92 And when he desired to migrate from this world to go to the mercy of the living God (Jn 13:1), he begged and prayed, implored and said: "Choose, O Lord, the one in whom I must transfer my spirit." He said to him: "Pour your spirit upon such-and-such!" Afterward, the one who was to sit on his seat migrated and went to a distant country, a country in which he could not have news of his relatives, and he reached the borders of Ethiopia. Abuna Damyanos prayed again to be able to know what would happen after him and who would ascend his seat. And our Lord said to Abuna Damyanos: "Do not fear, O my servant Damyanos, and do not sadden your heart on account of that son who has moved to a foreign country! He has not in fact gone away to seek the advantages of this world but to seek my ways, my precepts, and my ordinances. I have taken him by my will and I will make him return by my will and I will make him sit on your seat after you and I will make his blessing great. And I will accompany him wherever he goes and I will put my Spirit upon him (Is 61:1-2; Lk 4:18) and I will be with him always and I will not abandon him in his coming in and in his going out (Dt 28:6)."

with righteousness and He will answer you, draw near to Him and He will be near to you, trust in Him and He will fulfill for you (Ps 36:5)." 95 And after two years, that man returned from the place where he had emigrated when the brothers who were (emigrated) with him forced him, saying: "We are going to our country but you say: 'I will not come where I hear news of my relatives.' If they ask us: 'What happened to such-and-such, what became of him?', what can we say to them on your account? Shall we tell them that you are dead while you are not dead? And if we tell them: 'We left him (there)', we will become the laughingstock of neighbors and strangers." And while they were saying this, tears flowed from their eyes. When he saw the bitterness of their weeping, he said to them: "Brothers, why do you break my heart with such words (Acts 21:13)? If you really do not want to leave me, let us place a cross upon the altar and let us let the Lord make His choice and I will do what He asks me to do." They said to him: "Let it happen as you say." And they placed a cross upon the altar and together they prayed: "O Lord, you who search the heart, reveal!" (Acts 1:24). And there came (a voice?) that said: "Return to your country." And he returned on account of this word, and it was God Himself who had permitted this, so that the promise that God had made to Abuna Damyanos would be fulfilled. Abuna Demyanos receives an invitation from the king and sets out on his journey

96 Let us therefore recount and proclaim the migration of Abuna Damyanos. After that son [had returned], a message arrived to Abuna Damyanos from the king, who said to him: "Come to us and do not be lazy. We have in fact heard about you that you are a wise man, that you know the Scriptures, those ancient and those new. We desire to hear your word; we know that you do not crave the court, and yet you will know that if your coming is of benefit to the royal house, it will be advantageous also for all the subjects of the king. We too want to regulate ourselves according to the provisions of the Books so that we may obtain from the Lord what is good, together with all our subjects. We do not say this only to you, but we will summon all the learned of Ethiopia, and we have made you swear in the name of the Trinity, our God, so that you would not deny yourself to us (to come)." And (the messengers) said many other things to him.

ኢያምሕሉኒ ፡ ወአነኒ ፤ ኢይብሎሙ ፡ ኦሆ ። እስመ ፡፣ ፈራህክዎ ፡ ለቤተ ፡ ንጉሥ ፡ እስመ ፡ ጽሑፍ ፡ ዘይብል ፡ ኢትባእ ፡ ውስተ ፡ ባሕር ፡ ዘኢትክል ፡ ፀቢተ ። ወይእዜሰ ፡ ኦሆ ፡ ይኩን ፡ ፈቃደ ፡ እግዚአብሔር ፡ እስመ ፡ አልብየ ፡ ምክንያት” ፡ በዘ ፡ አአቢ”ሾ ፡ ምንተ ፡ እብል ፡ ወማሕላኒ ፡ እንተ ፡ አምሐለኒ ፡ እፈርህ ፡ ኪያሃ ።
told me such a thing and they should not have made me take such an oath so that I would not decline their request. I have in fact fear of the royal house because there is a writing that says: 'Do not venture into a sea that you cannot cross by swimming.' But now, let the will of the Lord be done because I have no reason to refuse myself. What could I say? And I have fear of the oath that he made me swear."

### Abuna Demyanos calls his successor

98 Afterward, the venerable father Abuna Damyanos decided to transmit his spirit to that man whom God had indicated to him, and in the midst of the assembly he said to him: "Talel." He answered: "What do you say, O my Lord?" He said to him: "My son, you know that I must undertake a long journey and I will return if the Lord wills it. Guard your brothers and feed them with the staff of justice. Do not refuse my order and my word because you know that our Lord said to our fathers the Apostles: 'He who hears you hears me, and he who opposes you opposes me and opposes Him who sent me' (Mt 10:40). Aware of this, say yes to me, so that my journey may go well." Having heard this, that man wept a great weeping and said: "What is this that I am hearing from you today, O father? This thing is not for me, who do not know my coming in and my going out (1 Kgs 3:7) and who, compared to all my brothers, resemble an abortion (1 Cor 15:8)." And while he was saying this, he made his tears flow like water, because he did not understand that all this was happening by the will of God and that precisely for this (God) had led him back from where he had migrated.

99 And Abuna Damyanos said to that man: "My son, why are you refusing my word? Will disobedience to the command of the master not perhaps be counted as a fault? If it had not been a sin, how come Joshua did not oppose Moses (Dt 31:7), Elisha Elijah (2 Kgs 2:1-15)? And why ever did Peter not oppose our Lord when (at the lake of) Tiberias (Jn 21:15-17) He said to him: 'Simon, son of Jonah, do you love me more than these?' and he answered 'O Lord, you know it, you are certain that I love you!' and our Lord said to him: 'Feed then my little sheep!' And how come he did not refuse? And why did Clement not oppose Peter when he gave him in charge the administration until the ends of the earth and gave him the number of the Eighty-One Books so that he would guide his house, the Church of God (1 Tim 3:15). Does our Lord not say: 'He who hears you, hears me and hears Him who sent me. And he who rejects you, rejects me and rejects


[p. 214] {#po.50.p214}
ሊተ ፡ አበየ ፡ አበዮ ፡ ለዘፈነወኒ ፡ ወአንተኒ ፡ ኢትእበየኒ ። ኦወልድየ ፡ እስመ ፡ እምኅበ ፡ እግዚአብሔር ፤ ተውህበ ፡ ለከ ፡ ወኦሆ ፡ ይቤሎ ፡ ከመ ፡ ይትፈጸም ፡ በላዕሌሁ ፡ ቃለ ፡ እግዚአብሔር ፡ ወነሥአ ፡ በረከቶ ፡ ወውእቱኒ ፡ ነሥአ ፡ እምነ ፡ ደቂቁ ፡ እለ ፡ ኀረየ ፡ ከመ ፡ ይትልውዎ።
Him who sent me' (Mt 10:40)? And you too, my son, do not refuse yourself to me, because (this) has been given to you by God." (That man) accepted so that the word of God would be fulfilled in him, and he obtained his blessing. And he too received (the blessing) from his children whom he had chosen so that they would follow him.

### Abuna Demyanos takes leave of his community

100 He set out on his journey and this became a motive of great mourning for his children. And the great and small emitted a great lament because he loved them and strove for the well-being of their souls. The adult said: "He loves me," and the young man said likewise: "Love me too more than anyone else!" In all his ways he made no distinction (between people). And when he admonished them, he did so using the staff that is used with one's own children and not with strangers (Heb 12:7): he in fact loved his children. They gave him their farewell and he took leave of them, saying: "My children, may the Lord be with you all! Live in love and in concord so that you may appear joyful as children of our father Paul. Abstain from fornication and from impurity that destroys the soul. And do not render it without fruit so that you do not become like dry wood: in fact, dry wood is good for nothing except to be cut and thrown into the fire (Jn 15:6). Be rather like the tree planted along the watercourses that gives fruit in its time (Ps 1:3) and respect your elders. And do not forget me in every Eucharistic celebration and in prayer so that I may be saved by your prayer; for the rest, I will give you instructions when I return" (1 Cor 11:34). Abuna Demyanos reaches the Nabura 3d Nob

101 Having said this, he set out and lived in a country that was under the jurisdiction of the Naburà 3d Nob. He had in fact been the latter's tutor because he was his brother's son and loved him as much as himself (Mt 19:19). He had indeed been his tutor like a mother and had raised him, imparting to him a good education and bestowing admonitions upon him, so that he became wise in all his ways, as it is written: "The educated son will be praised in the midst of the assembly." In the same manner, that man was tested with every discipline and acquired much wisdom, and precisely because of his wisdom, princes and kings loved him, and God also loved him and favored him wherever he went. He was indeed a shoot of the righteous, and as David says: "The descendants of the righteous will be blessed. Honor and wealth in his house, and his holiness will last forever" (Ps 111:2-3).

your offspring who will be born from your (womb), and I will prepare a kingdom for him, and he will build a house in my name. I will make his throne stable forever, and I will be a father to him, and he will be a son to me; and if sin should loom over him, I will punish him with the rod to be used with children and with the chastisement reserved for the sons of men. Nevertheless, I will not remove my mercy from him" (2 Sam 7:12-15). From this, therefore, we understood that the generation of the righteous is blessed in this way. A few days later, Abunà Damyanos said to the Neburà 3d Nob: "My son, how shall I go to the imperial court? I am afraid. But I also fear the oath that he made me swear. Tell me how I must behave!" 103 Master Nob answered and said to his father Damyanos: "O Abba, you have spoken rightly, and I too will answer you with honesty. [Does our Lord not tell us in the Gospel of Matthew, when he teaches us: 'A city built on a hill cannot remain hidden, and a lamp is not lit to cover it with a bushel basket but to put it on a lampstand so that it may give light to all who are in the house. Thus let your light shine [before men so that they may see your good works and give thanks to your Father who is in heaven' (Mt 5:14-16)? Is there not another passage where he says: 'Is there anyone who holds the plow and looks back?' (Lk 9:62)? The road of the Lord is leveled; hasten to go, for you have the gift of teaching." These things said the Naburà 3d Nob to Abunà Damyanos. And he took him with him because he wanted to be satisfied with the goodness of his word. In fact, he never set out on a journey in vain, but while he walked, he announced the word of God, more lovable than gold and silver, tastier than the honeycomb and honey (Ps 18:10). The Naburà 3d Nob rejoiced at every word he heard from him. The apprehension of Abunà Damyanos
ወበጽሑ ፡ ውስተ ፡ ዴዴ ፡ ንጉሥ ፡ ወጸለየ ፡ ወይቤ ፡ እግዚኦ ፡ እመሰ ፡ ታፈቅ ረኒ ፡ ተመጠዋ ፡ ለነፍስየ ። እስመ ፡ እፈርህ ፡ ነቢረ ፡ ውስተ ፡ ዴዴ ፡ ንጉሥ ፡ እስመ ፡ ሰፋጢት ፡ ይእቲ ፡ ወሰሐበቶሙ ፡ ለብዙኃን ፡ ጻድቃን ፡ ውስተ ፡ ፍናወ!" ፡ ዚአሃ፣ ወተአስሮሙ ፡ በአሕባሊሃ ፡ ወትወድዮሙ ፡ ውስተ ፡፤ መዛግብቲሃ ፡ ወታለብሶሙ ፡ ዓጽፈ ፡ ሙስናሃ ፤ ወታጸድፎሙ ፡ ውስተ ፡ መዓምቅቲሃ ፡ ወኢታበውሖሙ ፡ ይጻኡ ፡ እምኔሃ ። ወበእንተዝ ፡ እፈርህ ፡ ኪያሃ ፡ እግዚኦ ፡ አድኅነኒ ፡ እምኔሃ ፡ ወተመጠዋ ፡ ለነፍስየ ፡ ኀቤከ ፡ ኀበ ፡ ብዙኃ ፡ ሣህል ። ወኢትትሀየያ ፡ ለነፍስየ ፡ አላ ፡ ተመጠዋ ፡ ለስእለትየ ። ወዘንተ ፡ ብሂሎ ፡ ሰገደ ፡ ውስተ ፡ ምድር ፡ በገጹ ፡ ወገብአ ፡ ውስተ ፤ ማኅደሩ።
104 They arrived then at the king's court, and he prayed and said: "O Lord, if you love me, take my soul. I am indeed afraid of living in the king's court, for it is seductive and has dragged many righteous people toward its ways. And it binds them with its chains and puts them at its disposal, clothes them with the mantle of its perdition, and makes them plunge into its abysses, and does not allow them to emerge from them. For this reason, I am afraid of it. O Lord, deliver me from it and take my soul; take it to yourself in your great mercy! Do not despise my soul, but accept my supplication!". Having said this, he threw himself to the ground with his forehead in adoration and retired to his lodging. Abunà Damyanos reassured by a vision

water (Mt 10:42), will be saved by your merit". 106 When he heard this word of consolation, he rejoiced in his spirit and said: "I thank you, O my Lord, who have shown grace to your servant. Bring my body back, however, to my monastery, for even that which is far is near to you, and that which is difficult is an easy thing for you". And our Savior said to him: "Let it be done according to what you have said; I will not allow your body to be carried away by strangers, but only by your children whom you have gathered through the preaching of tongues. When, through the mouth of the messenger, they learn of the moment of your death, your children will come, and a great portent will occur in your flesh". Having said this, our Lord ascended into heaven in great glory (Acts 1:9). Last recommendations of Abunà Damyanos

107 After this, Abunà Damyanos said to his children who were with him: "My children, prepare yourselves, for the messenger from the king has arrived". They believed that he was speaking to them about the embassy of the earthly king. But he added: "From now on, there will no longer be a stay in this world. Be ready and light your lamps so that you may go out to receive the king (Mt 25:1)". This meant: "If you have any stains for which the enemy could accuse you, purify your soul".

### Death of Abunà Damyanos and his companions

108 At that juncture, they understood that he was speaking of death. They prepared themselves, therefore, by purifying themselves, and they waited to welcome the king, keeping their lamps lit like the five wise (virgins) (Mt 25:1ff). They waited dressed so as not to be caught naked. They all died except one, whom God spared so that he could recount what happened. Lastly, the venerable father, the Mar, rested. Funeral hymn

furnace, tested like gold (Pr 17:3; Ez 22:20, 22). From lips alien to vain speeches. Apples filled with spirit (Ct 2:5). Turtledove (Ct 2:12; Is 38:14) of the morning, chirping in the midst of the dense assembly. Why has such a father passed away? Teacher of the nations and of the people. How has such a fluent tongue ceased to speak? Spring of milk! Why ever does the follower of the Sun and morning star (Rev 2:28) fall silent! How did death manage to make the ear of this grain shoot wither, which the scorching of the dry season had not managed to make fade? Why is the herald of the new Gospel silent? The pure in body and soul like the sea of Tarsus! How is it that the organ of joy of the temple is silent? How did you remain, John, when you saw his eyes looking dimmed in their socket? What became of you, O John, when that fluent tongue was barred and ceased to speak? How did you manage to close the lamp under the bushel (Mt 5:15)? In what way were the bowels of your belly stirred when you remained alone and orphaned in a foreign country of which you did not even know the idiom? With whom did you console yourself? At whose house did you find a little rest? John buries Abunà Damyanos and returns to his monastery

110 Let us now move on to speak about how he buried him. When he passed away, he said: "O my father! Pains seize me like those of a woman in labor (Is 66:7; Jer 22:23)! Trembling and terror seize me. I do not know what to do. I gathered my strength and said to my soul: 'This terror will not bring you any benefit.' I collected my thoughts and buried my father in a secluded place and said: 'What good does it do me to weep alone? I will return to my country and tell my brothers that I have been left an orphan!'" Having said this, he rose and reached the country. They asked him: "Is everything well?". He answered them: "What good? If I have been left alone, of what good can one speak? Death has blocked the light of my eyes! What good, if my lamp has been put under the bushel (Mt 5:15)? How can I be saved, if my sun has become darkness in broad daylight? What good, if the diadem of my glory and my boast has fallen from my head? What good, now that my gem has been taken away, the precious stone in which I gloried, my jewel and ornament? 111 "What good, when I abandoned as if it were trash my ship that carries all my most precious treasures, for which my

poverty was defeated and I became rich together with my own? What good, if an inconsolable misfortune has fallen upon me and I am girded with sackcloth? Poor me, poor me, because our father of the fragrant tongue has died! Poor me, poor me, because our father of the fluent tongue has died! Woe to me! Poor you! Your lamp has indeed been covered under the bushel (Mt 5:15). Woe to me! Poor you! The sun that shone in the place of darkness has indeed been obscured (Is 9:1; Lk 1:78-79; 2 Pt 1:20). Woe to me! Poor me! The shepherd is missing and his sheep have scattered (Zec 13:7; Mt 26:31). Woe to me! Poor you! Behold, I announce to you an ominous message that will shake your heart and break your loins. Woe to me and woe to you! The song of your glory has been torn away. Woe to me and woe to you! Your ancient joy has been turned into mourning, and the condition of orphans besieges you like those who preceded you". And while he was saying this, he let tears flow from his eyes.

The children of Abunà Damyanos mourn the death of their founder 112 And when his children heard the news of his death, there were those who fell and those who rolled on the ground. There were those who screamed and those who cried out in a loud voice, saying: "Woe to us, poor us! Our father, fragrant tongue. Woe to us, poor us! Our father, filled with meekness. Woe to us, poor us! Our father, sun of the nations. Woe to us, poor us! The light of our glory has been buried and put under the bushel (Mt 5:15). Woe to us, poor us! Our father, the herald of the Gospel, has fallen silent, has ceased to speak. Woe to us, poor us! The legs of our father, which used to go to the east and to the west, to the north and to the south to summon souls to the knowledge of God, have been bound and have ceased to walk. Woe to us, poor us! The sailor has died and the treasures of the ship have scattered. Woe to us, poor us! The shepherd has died and his sheep remain unguarded (Zec 13:7; Mt 26:31). Woe to us, poor us! The diadem of our glory has indeed fallen. Woe to us, poor us! From now on we have become orphans;

woe to us, poor us! The pain that has no consolation grips us. 113 "Father, father! Light of the nations. Father, father! Shining lamp of the Church. Father, father! Joyful like the vines of Sorek (Is 5:2; Jer 2:21). Father, father! Meek like a lamb (Is 53:7; Jn 1:29). Father, father! Sea of humility. Father, father! Abyss of mercy. Woe to us, poor us! You have therefore chosen a foreign country (to die in). Woe to us, poor us! To whom have you left your sheep? Woe to us, poor us! Those lips of yours that sprinkled the hyssop of the Spirit (Ps 51:9) have been submerged by the stones of death. Woe to us, poor us! Those lips of yours that proclaimed the good news have therefore been covered with the dust of death. Woe to us, poor us! Our joy is finished, and the song of our glory has been torn away. The diadem of our boast has fallen". And while they were saying these things,

"ዘከመዝ ፡ ነገረ” ፡ ሰቆቃው ፡ ወላሐ”' ፡ ብካይ” ፡ ብዙኀ ፡ መዋዕለ ፡ እንዘ ፡ ይላሕዉ ፡ ዎ” ፡ ለአቡሆሙ ። ወመኑ ፡ ሰሚዖ ፡ ሰቆቃወ ፡ እሉ ፡ ዘኢያውሕዝ ፡ አንብዖ ፡ እመ፣ ኢኮነ ፡ ዘከማየ ፡ ዕንቡዘ ፡ ሕሊና ። ወለሐውዎ ፡ ብዙሐ ፡ መዋዕለ ።
they cried at the top of their lungs, screamed and rolled on the ground, slapped their faces and tore their clothes, and they stood thus, raising laments and weeping for many days, mourning for their father. And who, if not someone like me with a weak conscience, hearing those laments of theirs, would not have shed his tears? And they mourned him for several days.

### On the trail of the remains of Abunà Damyanos
ወእምድኅረ ፡ ኀልቀ ፡ መዋዕለ ፡ ላሖሙ ፡ ተባሀሉ” ፡ በበይናቲሆሙ ፡ ንዑ ፡ ንኅሥሥ ፡ መድፍነ” ፡ ዘተኀጥአ ፡ ንዑ ፡ ንኅሥዖሦ ፡ ለልሳነ ፡ ዘርብ ፡ እስመ ፡ ኢርቱዕ ፡ ይትከዓው ፡ ጸበለ” ፣ ሥጋሁ ፡ ውስተ ፡ ብሔረ ፡ ባዕድ ፡ ንዑ ፥ ንኅሥዖሦ ፡ ለአበ ፡ መንፈስነ ፡ እስመ ፡ ንረክቦ ፡ ለእመ ፡ አጥባዕነ ። ወዘንተ ፣ ብሂሎሙ ፡ ወፅኡ ፡ ለቀ ቢረ” ፡ አቡሆሙ ፡ ከመ ፡ ይኅሥሥዎ ፡ ዘእንበለ ፡ ኑፋቄ ፡ ወበእንተዝ ፣፤ ረድኦሙ ፡ ረድኤተ ፡ ጸሎቱ ፡ ለአቡሆሙ ።
114 And after the period of their mourning was finished, they said to one another: "Come, let us seek the treasure that has gone missing! Come, let us seek (the father of) the fluent tongue! It is not right, in fact, that the dust of his flesh should be scattered in a foreign land. Come, let us seek the father of our spirit! For if we are determined, we will find him". Having said this, they went out without hesitation to seek their father and to give him burial. And in this, the help of their father's prayer came to their aid.

### Attempt to steal the body of Abunà Damyanos
ወሶበ ፡ በጽሑ” ፡ መካን ፡ ኅበ ፡ ተቀብረ ፡ አቡነ ፡ ድምያኖስ ፡ ወድቁ ፡ ወበ ከዩ ፡ ብዙኃ ፡ በኅቡእ ፡ ወአኮ ፡ ዘይፈቅዱ ፤ ክሥተ ፡ ይኩኖሙ ፡ እስመ ፡፣ ዴዴ ፡ ንጉሥ ፡ ውእቱ ፡ ወወዓሉ ፡ ይእተ” ፡ ዕለተ ፤ እስከ ፡ ይመሲ ። ወከዊኖ ፡ ምሴተ ፡ በጽማዌ ፡ ሌሊት ፡ ከሠቱ ፣፤ መቃብሪሁ ፡ ለብእሴ ፡ እግዚአብሔር ፡ ወቆመ ፡ ብርሃነ ፡ ማኅቶት ፡ ወአብርሃ ፡ ሎሙ ፡ ወወፅአ ፡ መዓዛ ፡ ሠናይ ፡ እምውስተ ፡ ሥጋሁ ፡ዘአልቦ ፡ ዘአመ” ፡ ፄነወ ፤ ዘከማሁ ፡ ፄና ፤ መዓዛ ፡ ሠናይ ። ወነሥኡ ፤ ሥጋሁ ፡ ለአቡነ ፡ ድምያኖስ ፡ ወአልቦ ፡ ዘነከየ ፡ ወኢምንተኒ ፡ አላ ፡ ረከብዎ ፡ ከመ ፡ ዘዮም ፡ ተገንዘ ። ወወደይዎ ፡ ውስተ ፡ ሣፁን ፡ ወአንበርዎ ፡ ታሕተ ፡ አሐቲ ፡ ዕፅ ፡ እስከ ፡ ይከውን ፡ ጽማዌ ፡ ሌሊት ፡ ወየሐውሩ” ፡ በሌሊት ።
115 And when they arrived at the place where Abunà Damyanos had been buried, they fell and wept for a long time in secret. They did not want their intentions (to remove the body) to be discovered, because it was the king's court, and that day they waited until evening came. When it was evening, in the silence of the night, they opened the tomb of the man of God, and a light rose up and illuminated them. And from his flesh, a sweet fragrance came out. Never was there such a sweet fragrance, since there has been a sense of smell. They then took the body of Abunà Damyanos and (saw that) nothing was missing from him at all. Rather, they found him (so intact) as if he had been wrapped that very day. They then put him in a chest and deposited him under a plant until the night was well advanced, to then set out on their journey, precisely during the night. The king notices and reproaches the disciples of Abunà Damyanos

116 When it was the sixth hour of the day, the king's emissaries arrived and said to them: "Come!". And they pressed them. They said: "Father! Father! Come with us, precede us and you fix things". Those emissaries of the king led them in haste and brought them before the king. And the king roared like a lion, for he was a lion of the nations. He said to them: "O men, how did you dare to violate my court and dared to carry away my spiritual father!". And he reproached them for many other things. And those men answered the king with a terrified voice and said to him: "O king, our lord, you know everything and understand every thing. Does not all of Ethiopia belong to you? Even the water we drink belongs to you! (All this) God has given you. And our father did not come to you attracted by riches, but because of the invitation you addressed to him, when you said to him: 'Come to me!' And he came to you against his will, (only) because the


[p. 226] {#po.50.p226}
ወሖረ ፡ ኅበ ፡ ዘለዓለም ፡ ንጉሥ ። ወይእዚኒ ፡ አብሐነ ፡ ንቅብሮ ፡ ለዘዚአከ ፡ ኀበ ፡ ዘዚአከ ፡ እስመ ፡ እንቲአከ ፡ ይእቲ ፡ ኩላ ፡ ምድር ፡ እስከ ፡ ማይ ፡ ዘንሰቲ ፡ በከመ ፡ ንቤ።
king's command is inviolable. But then what God established happened, and he went to the eternal King. And now allow us to bury your (dear one) in our country. Yours is in fact all the land, as we have already said, down to the water we drink".

### The king grants the translation of the body of Abunà Damyanos
ወዘንተ ፡ ሰሚዖ ፡ ንጉሥ ፡ ጌረወ ፡ ወምሕሮሙ ፡ ወይቤሎሙ ፡ ቅብሩ ፡ አባ ክሙ፡ ፥ ኀበ ፡ ፈቀድክሙ ። ወተፈሥሑ ፡ ሶቤሃ ፡ ወአእመሩ ፡ ከመ ፡ ኢኮነ ፡ ርጉቀ ፡ እምኔሆሙ ፡ ረድኤተ ፡ አቡሆሙ ፡ ወነሥኡ ፡ ዘአፍቀሩ ፡ ሥጋ ፡ ወአእተውዎ ፡ ኅበ ፡ ዘጸውዓ ፡ እንግልጋ ። እንዘ ፡ ይረድኦሙ ፡ እንበለ ፡ ንትጋ ፡ ዘተወፈዩ ፡ ሀብተ ፡ጸጋ ፣ በረከተ ፡ ጸሎቱ ፡ ተሃሉ ፡ ምስሌነ”' ፡ አሜን ። ወኮነ ፡ ዕረፍቱ” ፣ ለአቡነ ፡ ድም ያኖስ ፡ አመ ፡ ፳ ወቿ ለወርኅ ፡ የካቲት ፡ በረከተ ፡ ጸሎቱ ፡ ወፍተ ፡ በረከቱ ፡ ወኃይለ ፡ ጸሎቱቴ” ፡ ተሀሉ ፡ ምስሌነ፤ ፡ አሜን ። ወአሜን” ።
117 Having heard this, the king softened and forgave them and said to them: "Bury your father where you wish". Then they rejoiced and understood that the help of their father was not far from them. They then took that body which they had loved and brought it back to the people whom he had gathered and whom he helped without letting anything be missing, who had received the wealth of grace. May the blessing of his prayer remain with us. Amen! The rest of our father Damyanos took place on the twenty-eighth day of the month of Yàkkatit. May the blessing of his prayer, the crumbs of his superabundance, and the power of his intercession remain with us. Amen! FE Amen!

118 After his death, Abunà Damyanos sent a message to his disciples of Dàbrà Sahl. A recluse who lived in the hermitage of Habàla said: "Do not believe that your father Damyanos is dead; he has rather hidden himself from your sight. I myself saw him with my own eyes standing before the Lord of spirits. In his hand he held a golden cross, and without shame he said to the Lord of spirits: 'Give me my reward, as you gave it to the Apostles. There is nothing in which I have been found less than the Apostles.' And the Lord of spirits said: 'You truly have merit, receive what you have asked for!' And he granted him what he had given to the Apostles. After this, he said to me: 'Go to my children and tell them what you have seen. And warn them, saying to them: Keep yourselves ready to receive the wealth of grace and keep yourselves pure from fornication and impurity, love one another, and do not transgress the word of your master!'". These things said the recluse, and he passed by and went on his way and re-entered his retreat. May his blessing remain with us. Amen! Abunà Damyanos urges the search for the lost sheep 119 Abunà Damyanos sent a message again regarding the lost sheep and said: "From the venerable father Abunà Yonas. Why do you scatter my sheep that I have gathered through the preaching of the Gospel? And now

ወኅሥሥ ፡ ወአግብእ ። ወሶበ ፡ ሰምዐ ፡ አበ ፡ ምኔት ፡ ይቤ ፡ አስተጋብኡ ፡ ወደገመ ፡ ብሂለ ፤ ውእቱ ፡ ላእክ፤ ፡ ኪያሃ ፡ ቃለ ፡ ከመ ፣ ቀዳሚ ። አስተሐየጹ” ፡ መነኮሳት ፡ ዘዘዚአሆሙ ፡ ወይቤሉ ፡ እገሊት ፡ ኢሀለወት ፡ ውስተ ፡ ምኔተ ፡ መበለታት ፡ ወይቤ ፡ አበ ፡ ምኔት ፡ ሖሩ! ፡ ኅሠ ፡ ወአምጽኡ ፡ ወአእትውዋ ፡ ውስተ ፡ ምኔተ ፡ መበለታት ። ወብዙኃን ፡ አንከሩ ፡ እምዝ ፡ ተአምር ፡ ወአፈድፈዱ ፡ አክብሮቶ ፡ በሞቱ ፡ እምነ ፡ በሕይወቱ ።
move without delay and go to seek and bring back (to the fold)". And when the abbot heard this, he said: "Gather!". And [this minister continued to repeat the same word as at the beginning. | The monks looked around in search and said: "Such and such (a person) is not present in the convent of the nuns". The abbot said: "Go, seek her and bring her back to the convent of the nuns". And many marveled at this miracle and venerated him much more now that he was dead than when he was alive.

### Epilogue
ወተአምሪሁሰ ፡ ዘገብረ ፡ ኩሎ ፡ ለእመ ፡ ተጽሕፈ ፡ እመ ፡ ኢያግመሮ ፡ ብራና ። ወዝ ፡ ጸሐፍኩ ፡ በአምጣነ" ፡ ክህልኩ ፡ ለእመቦ” ፡ ዘገደፍኩ ፡ ወዘወሰኩ ፡ ወዘገንጳ ልኩ ፡ ወለእመ ፡ ኢያርታዕኩ ፡ ጾታ ፡ ነገር ፡ አርትዑ ፡ በቃልክሙ ፡ እለ ፡ ብክሙ ፡ አእምሮ ። ወኢትትዓቀፉ ፡ ብየ ፡ እስመ ፡ ውድ ፡ አእምሮትየ ። ወአልብየ ፡ ክሂለ ፡ አሣእስኦ ፡ ነገር ፡ እስመ ፡ ብእሲ ፡ ነዳየ ፡ አእምሮ ፡ አነ ። ወባሕቱ ፡ በእንተ ፡ ዘአ ጽሀቀኒ ፡ ፍቅረ ፡ ዚአሁ ፡ ጸሐፍኩ ። ጸሎቱ ፡ ወበረከቱ ፡ ተሀሉ ፡ ምስሌነ" ፡ አሜን ። ወአሜን" ። ለይኩን ። ስብሐት ፡ ለእግዚአብሔር ፡ ለወጢን ፡ ዘያነቅህ ። ወኅበ ፡ ፍጻሜ ፡ ዘያበጽሕ ። ለዓለመ ፡ ዓለም ፡ አሜን ። ወአሜን፤!! ። ለዘጸሐፎ ፡ ወለዘአጽሐፎ ፡ ወለዘአንበቦ ። ወለዘሰምዓ ፡ ቃላቲሁ ፡ ኀቡረ ፡ ይምሐሮሙ" ፡ እግዚአብሔር ፡ በመ ንግሥተ ፡ ሰማያት ፡ አሜን ። ወአሜን ፡ ለይኩን ። 'ወአቡነ ፡ ጳውሎስ" ፡ ዘአጽሐፈ ፡ ዘንተ ፡ መጽሐፈ ፡ ገድሉ ፡ ለአቡነ ፡ ድምያኖስ' ፡ ይጽሐፍ ፡ ስሞ” ፡ እግዚአብሔር ፡ ሊቅ ፡ ኅበ ፡ ዓምደ ፡ ወርቅ ። በቀለመ ፡ ጽድቅ ። ወያስተፍሥሖ ፡ ምስለ ፡ ደቂቁ ፡ ወደቂቀ ፡ ደቂቁ" ፡ በኢየሩሳሌም ፡ ሐዳስ ፡ አመ ፡ ይገብር ፡ ለኅሩያን ፡ ምርፋቀ"! ። አሜን”።
120 And if one were to put into writing all the miracles he performed, there would not be enough parchment to contain them (Jn 21:25). I have written this within the limits of my capacity. If there is something I have omitted or added or turned upside down, if I have not followed a logical thread in my exposition, correct it when you read, O you who have intelligence! And do not be scandalized by me, because I am a person with limited knowledge and incapable of speaking with elegance, because I am poor in intelligence. I have written only because his love compelled me. May his prayer and his blessing remain with us. Amen! ΓΕ Amen! So be it. Glory to God who spurs to begin and who accompanies until the end, for ages of ages. Amen! ΓΕ Amen! May the Lord use mercy in the Kingdom of heaven with all those who have written and with those who have had it written, with those who read and with all who listen to his word. Amen! And Amen! So be it. And to our father Pawlos who had this book of the life of Abunà Damyanos written, may the great Lord write his name in the golden column, with ink of justice. May he make him rejoice together with his children and the children of his children in the new Jerusalem (Rev 21:2), when he prepares the table for his elect (Mt 22:1-14; Rev 19:9). Amen!
